For a report bound to the referenced node `surface:algorithm`, bind N=3 in
`Report.models.surface.parameters`; retain every hardware/code-distance
value from the target, and set `shared:[]`. This changes the report's bytes,
not the target. N left null yields a symbolic repeat with duration
`N*30 ns`, count charge `N/10^7`, and occupancy `N*1500 physical-qubit·ns`.
Its peak is piecewise: 0 for N=0, 50 for positive N. Do not simplify it
to 50 without retaining the N>0 premise. A fixed distance selection d=3
instead creates new target bytes and requires recomputing `Binding.value`
as `9/500000000` seconds; an old 30 ns resolved value must reject.

| Bound instance / composition | Duration | Physical peak | Physical occupancy | Additive risk charge |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| One cycle, d=5 | 30 ns | 50 | 1500 qubit·ns | 1/10^7 |
| Three sequential cycles, d=5 | 90 ns | 50 | 4500 qubit·ns | 3/10^7 |
| Three independent parallel cycles, d=5 | 30 ns | 150 | 4500 qubit·ns | 3/10^7 |
| Three sequential cycles, d=3 | 54 ns | 18 | 972 qubit·ns | 3/10^5 |
| Zero repetitions | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

The complete entry above converts cycle 30 ns to `3/100000000` seconds
using factor `1/1000000000`. Its physical quantity is 50
physical_qubit/logical_qubit, not 50 physical qubits until multiplied by
one logical qubit in the leaf interval. The risk formula is an illustrative
external physical-model assumption. Even when its risk is positive, the
logical gate's exact `epsilon:0` stays 0, and any checked oracle fault rate
is independently obtained from F_B and its declared domain.

For a shared-factory variant remove its private physical interval, add
`shared_uses:[{id:"factory0",slots:"1"}]` to each factory-using leaf, and
declare one typed shared reservation in the report:

```json
{"id":"factory0","model":"surface","pool":"factory","unit":"physical","start":{"lit":"0","unit":"ns"},"end":{"lit":"90","unit":"ns"},"amount":{"lit":"50","unit":"physical"},"slots":"1"}
```

Three sequential 30 ns uses fit that reservation: peak 50 and occupancy
4500 physical-qubit·ns, with no threefold factory space charge. Three
parallel uses of a one-slot factory are infeasible. A model that actually
supplies a three-slot factory may reserve 50 physical qubits for 30 ns
and assert capacity 3 as an external-model assumption; then peak 50 and
occupancy 1500 physical-qubit·ns are the checked **accounting**, not a proof
that such hardware exists. The private-data and factory pool names remain
distinct. The source and normalized model hashes are recomputed
by check_rev2.py; they are fixture identities, not published pins.

## Conformance vectors

These are acceptance obligations for the proposed format. “Error” below
means model/report validation or requested-schedule failure as specified,
never a new source-program trap or quantum oracle fault.

| Vector | Required verdict / result |
|---|---|
| Serialized example, d=5/N=3 | Valid import; 30 ns binding; sequential totals above. |
| d=3 with updated resolved value and hash | 18 ns/cycle, 18 physical/logical, risk 1/10^5. |
| Change d without updating Binding.value | Error at stale resolved value, before concrete scheduling. |
| d=4 under this model's declared odd domain; d=0 | Domain error. Another model declaring even distance with appropriate formulas remains legal. |
| Null N; N=0; N=3 | Symbolic repeat / empty composition / three copies, respectively. |
| Null hardware parameter used by a binding | Valid symbolic template; incomplete concrete schedule, naming that parameter; unrelated object execution unaffected. |
| Forward quantity reference; quantity/invoke cycle even under repeat 0 | Forward reference valid; either cycle error. |
| Division by zero; negative root; nonintegral pow exponent; zero^zero | Exact expression error. Integer negative powers such as 2^−3=1/8 valid. |
| sqrt(9/4); sqrt(2) | Exact 3/2; exact algebraic expression or verified outward enclosure. At 3 places sqrt(2) is enclosed by [1.414,1.415]. |
| Exact unit conversion factor `"1/3"` | Preserve the rational factor without rounding or a JSON binary64 restriction. |
| 1/3 with upper/lower at 3 places | 0.334/0.333; exact rational mode retains 1/3. |
| Add time and dimensionless risk; bind physical/logical ratio into time | Unit error, no heuristic unit/name conversion. |
| Unknown target path; path into epsilon; duplicate path; bad escaping | Binding error, before any target realization is used. |
| Source byte/hash mismatch; same name with another source version | Mismatch error; distinct identity, respectively. |
| Missing mapped quantity; unaligned quantity with newly declared model resource | Missing output error; valid preserved named resource, respectively. |
| Two quantities mapped to one aligned resource | Preserve both quantity-keyed displays; charge only the explicitly composed counts. |
| Unknown expression field / `var:eps` / accuracy bucket | Invalid normalized expression, never a model epsilon binding. |
| Unsupported source expression at a retained external location | Unchecked report with complete source/obligation; no invented value or algorithm rejection. |
| Three independent sequential/parallel cycles | Same count/occupancy, different makespan and peak as above. |
| Duplicate occurrence of one DAG child | Counts and private demand multiply; DAG storage sharing does not share a physical instance. |
| Shared factory sequence / parallel with one slot | One factory charged once / infeasible simultaneous use; serialized alternative remains legal. |
| Shared interval outside root time or not covering use | Error at reservation/use. Touching intervals have no overlap. |
| Cross-model symbolic invoke of an algorithm argument | Convert dimensions/units, retain caller dependency, qualify identities; no hardware override. |
| Report/certificate with wrong target, context or model hash | Reject the asserted report; no first-context or old-hash cache fallback. |
| Positive physical risk with exact logical gates | Risk output/charge remains nonzero; epsilon and logical fault claims unchanged. |
| Complete selector + complete ModelInput + concrete dependencies | Checked scalar under the ordinary objective rules. |
| Complete selector + well-formed ModelInput with free non-operational dependent parameters | Symbolic unresolved objective, with no invented value. |
| Missing required model_outputs selector | Validation error at objective.model_outputs.<resource>; not a symbolic input. |
| Missing required ModelInput | Explicit incomplete-model-objective diagnostic; no completed concrete optimization. |
| Root/model parameters/shared reservations changed in a certifying report | Reject if they differ from its schedule-bound ModelInput; no report-selected objective. |
| Algorithm parameter N operationally binds time, target N=1; report/invoke N=2 | Reject the changed operational value. A newly resolved N=2 target must have value 2 and a new hash. |
| Standalone X reports with 0 and 1 inherited controls | Explicit subjects differ; 1-second and 2-second estimates cannot be interchanged. |
| 90 ns one-slot shared factory, 50 versus 100 qubits, same selector | Legal inputs give peaks 50/100 and occupancy 9/2000000 versus 9/1000000 physical-qubit seconds, with distinct schedule hashes. |

The executable check_rev2.py covers the fixture's scalar arithmetic and
unit conversions; parameter/formula negative cases; forward reference and
zero-multiplied cycle; exact rational roots/powers and rational enclosure
inequalities; sequence/parallel/repeat/shared-accounting examples; hashes;
the seeded input-identity/freeze witnesses, versioned catalog hashes,
and document structure. Cross-model invokes, actual operational target
resolution, source adapter translation, generic algebraic evaluation,
context validation and the remaining consumer acceptance vectors remain **proposed**.
A small rational example checker is not an implementation of this format.

## Objective selector and input witness

Declare factory_peak as a model resource with physical_qubit dimension
and scale 1. Use the shared-factory variant specified above (its changed
nodes require its own recomputed model_hash), N=3, and this objective:

```json
{"weighted":{"factory_peak":1},"model_outputs":{"factory_peak":{"model":"surface","node":"algorithm","metric":"peak","pool":"[\"surface\",\"factory\"]"}}}
```

Its schedule.bindings.model_inputs.factory_peak contains root
`{model:"surface",node:"algorithm"}`, complete model/parameter bindings,
and the explicit shared reservation. With one slot over [0,90 ns),
reserving 50 or 100 physical qubits gives peak 50 or 100, and occupancy
9/2000000 or 9/1000000 physical-qubit seconds. Both inputs are legal and
have the same target/selector, but distinct schedule hashes. A report
for the 100-qubit reservation cannot certify the 50-qubit schedule input.
No external report selection supplies the reservation implicitly.

For the selector-validation vector, `{"weighted":{"factory_peak":1}}`
is malformed: objective.model_outputs.factory_peak is missing. The
complete selector above with a well-formed input retaining free N is
symbolic; bind N and compatible reservation endpoints to obtain a concrete
scalar. Missing ModelInput gives the separate incomplete-input diagnostic.
These are model/scheduling outcomes, never ISA fault codes. The test uses
a finite schematic schedule envelope to witness identity sensitivity;
publication still needs complete bound production fixtures.

## Consumer impact, release and what remains for 0.10

For 0.9 every fully bound invocation retains its §10.1 estimate of the
complete padded realisation, exact mandatory rows and checked certificate,
including unmodified SQLite at N=2^20. Materialise fully resolved operational
fields into ordinary scalar 0.9 targets, retaining original source, parameter
bindings and hierarchy as external provenance. Equivalent accounting need
not materialise every gate. estimate_nresources continues to enumerate only
scheduled count/time/occupancy/peak rows; new model-only resources use
model_estimates and add no scheduled scalar row. An unused model output
still remains visible in the model report. Preserve all promised algorithm scope. This is a
compatibility path, not completion of symbolic import support. Do not
pretend a new JSON field is already admitted by 0.9's closed schema. The
reference interpreter's current declared 0.8 surface also does not gain
0.10 admission through a documentation proposal.

For 0.10, target importers parse the pinned source version, preserve raw
bytes, normalize formulas/units/domains, and supply resolved bindings.
The scheduler selects distance using classical data, creates a distinct
resolved target, and evaluates model objectives without materializing all
gates. The estimator retains symbolic hierarchies and reports intervals,
shared factories and named quantities. The VM/circuit executors preserve
the ordinary logical machine semantics and use the resolved costs;
physical simulation is not inferred from a resource-risk output. The
independent checker verifies translation where supported, target/report
identity, arithmetic, accounting and truthful obligation labels. The
conformance harness must run all format vectors with separate producers
and checker; a consumer should not share a vendor importer as its sole
verification oracle across a published contract boundary.

No reference catalog recipe body, source instruction syntax or published
0.9 envelope is edited by this proposal. Under the normal full-version 0.10 publication, affected
semantic envelopes and source hashes change because their version changes;
do not promise a global no-repin migration. Target hashes change for new
models/resolved distances; target-bound schedules and certificates must
be re-bound and checked. Algorithms' estimate parameters and hierarchy
change the report/certificate bytes, independently of source identity.
Normal full-version 0.10 publication MUST also republish the default ref
catalog with isa="0.10" and version="0.10", update its §11.1 envelope and
§17 item 7 default binding, and recompute dependent schedule/certificate
identities and pins. All 219 recipe bodies may remain unchanged. The
metadata-only in-memory transformation gives:

```text
ref@0.9:  5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197
ref@0.10: 83cc9c177bad054bd7af35dc1892645d869b9e4c0b9c968f35b8783ce2b54c42
```

These are versioned envelope hashes, not raw-file SHA-256. The 0.10
calculation uses the published JCS routine, changing exactly the catalog's
isa/version and envelope's isa/version while preserving recipes. Published
0.9 artifacts stay unchanged; this job writes no catalog and re-pins no
artifact. Unchanged recipes do not imply an unchanged versioned identity.
Non-operational objective ModelInputs change schedule_hash; every
model report remains bound to its specific device object/root in the
mixed-fat-object inventory, not a native-program aggregate.

Remaining 0.10 work is concrete: council acceptance of these new
encodings and unit/accounting rules; machine-readable target/certificate
schemas and vectors; source-format/version adapters including retained
external expressions; an independent translation checker or versioned
checkable witness mechanism for each such expression; scalable symbolic
DAG evaluation, distance-search implementation and a versioned checkable witness for
optimality claims when requested; operational-resolution and context
validation in consumers; compatibility and publication tooling. Routing,
physical pulse/noise simulation and a general proof that vendor QEC models
predict hardware are separate mechanics/assumptions, not hidden obligations
this arithmetic proposal claims to solve. It supplies no automatic
independence law for physical failures and no physical probability to
logical-norm conversion. A user may import an explicit, separately checked
risk-composition formula as model data; no such capability is prohibited.

Keep **b-isa-spec-fd0** open for that contract/adapter work and
**b-targets-gyp** open at its owning repository until the promised complete
path exists. Preserving an unchecked external obligation is honest interim
reporting, not a non-narrowing substitute for implementing it. Neither bead
was mutated by this notes-only job. The exact new mechanism is described
above; no new cross-repo issue is necessary to name the same blocker.

name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. # Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# Agent Instructions

This project uses **bd** (beads) for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` for full workflow context.

> **Architecture in one line:** Issues live in a local Dolt database
> (`.beads/dolt/`); cross-machine sync uses `bd dolt push/pull` (a
> git-compatible protocol), stored under `refs/dolt/data` on your git
> remote — separate from `refs/heads/*` where your code lives.
> `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export, not the wire protocol.
>
> See [sync-concepts](https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md)
> for the one-screen overview and anti-patterns (don't treat JSONL as the
> source of truth; don't `bd import` during normal operation; don't
> reach for third-party Dolt hosting before trying the default).

## Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work atomically
bd close <id>         # Complete work
bd dolt push          # Push beads data to remote
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var

## Beads Issue Tracker

This project uses **bd (beads)** for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` to see full workflow context and commands.

### Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work

```

### Rules

- Use `bd` for ALL task tracking — do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown TODO lists
- Run `bd prime` for detailed command reference and session close protocol
- Use `bd remember` for persistent knowledge — do NOT use MEMORY.md files

**Architecture in one line:** issues live in a local Dolt DB; sync uses `refs/dolt/data` on your git remote; `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export. See https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md for details and anti-patterns.

## Agent Context Profiles

The managed Beads block is task-tracking guidance, not permission to override repository, user, or orchestrator instructions.

- **Conservative (default)**: Use `bd` for task tracking. Do not run git commits, git pushes, or Dolt remote sync unless explicitly asked. At handoff, report changed files, validation, and suggested next commands.
- **Minimal**: Keep tool instruction files as pointers to `bd prime`; use the same conservative git policy unless active instructions say otherwise.
- **Team-maintainer**: Only when the repository explicitly opts in, agents may close beads, run quality gates, commit, and push as part of session close. A current "do not commit" or "do not push" instruction still wins.

## Session Completion

This protocol applies when ending a Beads implementation workflow. It is subordinate to explicit user, repository, and orchestrator instructions.

1. **File issues for remaining work** - Create beads for anything that needs follow-up
2. **Run quality gates** (if code changed) - Tests, linters, builds
3. **Update issue status** - Close finished work, update in-progress items
4. **Handle git/sync by active profile**:
   ```bash
   # Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval.
   git status

   # Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
   git pull --rebase
   bd dolt push
   git push
   git status
   ```
5. **Hand off** - Summarize changes, validation, issue status, and any blocked sync/commit/push step

**Critical rules:**
- Explicit user or orchestrator instructions override this Beads block.
- Do not commit or push without clear authority from the active profile or the current user request.
- If a required sync or push is blocked, stop and report the exact command and error.

# fd0 — imported QEC models, parameters and hierarchy

Proposal for council review; `b-isa-spec-fd0`, originating at
`b-targets-gyp`. No published target or spec bytes are edited.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §8.3 imports maps a model quantity to `[resource,factor]`.
§8.1 has a closed expression language and binds no code distance,
hardware parameter or imported quantity. §8.2 aggregates scheduled leaf
events, not a factory hierarchy or a symbolic algorithm call graph.
There is no binding rule from an imported QEC formula to a logical gate's
time or qubit class. A numeric mapping is implemented by the supplied
bead's account; it does not supply these missing semantics.

North Star §7, **Qualtran, Azure RE**, promises symbolic hierarchical
counting and imported models as target data. §4.3 requires all declared
resources with their own units. This is an S0 contract scope gap for that
import capability, rather than permission to reject such algorithms.
Scalar materialisation is an available 0.9 path for a completely bound
instance; it is not the full symbolic contract. Keep fd0 as the explicit
blocker for that missing contract, even if the new encoding is 0.10.

Primary evidence: Microsoft's [QEC architecture model example](https://quantum.microsoft.com/en-us/insights/blogs/resource-estimation/modeling-quantum-architecture-with-azure-quantum-resource-estimator)
uses code distance and hardware timing in its cycle formula. Qualtran's
own [call graph protocol](https://qualtran.readthedocs.io/en/latest/resource_counting/call_graph.html)
admits symbolic multiplicities. Its [release notes](https://github.com/quantumlib/Qualtran/releases)
say AlgorithmSummary was removed in favor of GateCounts; adapters must pin
the input format/version, not assume the old class is current. These
sources were read as model-format evidence, not imported code.

## Alternatives and worked example

Use a declared odd code distance d=5, hardware measurement time τ=2 ns,
and **illustrative** formulas chosen for this witness:
`cycle=3τd`, `physical=2d²`, `logical_risk=(1/10)*(1/100)^((d+1)/2)`.
The results are 30 ns, 50 physical qubits per logical qubit and 1/10^7.
The first expression can be numerically precomputed; it cannot reference
d in current §8.1. The risk expression needs exponentiation. It is a
physical-model risk, not a bound on phase-sensitive operator norm.

For three sequential cycles with one logical qubit, the model gives
90 ns, peak 50 physical qubits and occupancy 4500 physical-qubit·ns.
For three simultaneous independent logical qubits held for one cycle,
time is 30 ns and peak is 150, with the same occupancy. Adding all scalar
quantities would get the parallel time or sequential peak wrong.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Bind d and hardware externally; emit a scalar 0.9 target | Sound concrete compatibility path; retain the original model and bindings in provenance. No symbolic selection or hierarchy is represented in the target. |
| Add parameters and formulas only | Solves evaluation, but not where a formula is consumed, hierarchical multiplicities, concurrency or double counting. Insufficient alone. |
| Bound target model plus separate hierarchical estimate (recommended) | Fix hardware/model choices in target identity; bind symbolic algorithm inputs and model accounting to the certificate; retain §8.2 leaf accounting separately. |
| Treat failure probability as gate epsilon or sum every quantity | Unsound error semantics or wrong units/aggregation. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

For the extended target/cert version, add §8.5 and a corresponding K entry:

```text
8.5 Imported model bindings
A target MAY contain model_parameters:{name:{value:number|null,unit:string}}
and each imports entry MAY additionally contain
{source_hash, source_format, parameters:{name:expr},
 quantities:{name:{unit,expr}}, bindings:{destination:quantity_name}}.
source_hash is SHA-256 of the source model's canonical bytes; source_format
includes a format version. References {param:name} resolve model_parameters;
{quantity:name} resolves a quantity of this model. Dependencies form a DAG;
cycles, duplicate definitions and undeclared references are invalid.
Formula expressions add pow(base,exponent) for integer exponents and
sqrt(x) for the nonnegative principal root, in model context only.
Rational operations and integer powers are exact; roots are exact
algebraic quantities, converted to a reported decimal only with a stated
outward bound. Division by zero, negative roots and nonfinite concrete
outputs are errors. No eps or accuracy-bucket variable is bound here.
Integer parameters declare their integer constraint; a code-distance
parameter additionally declares its permitted parity/range.
A null parameter is symbolic: an estimate retains it as a free parameter.
It MUST be bound before concrete target scheduling or numeric evaluation,
and every concrete binding is included in the resolved target's bytes.
Only an explicit bindings entry may substitute a model quantity for a
gate cost, class quantity or other target numeric field. Its destination
must name a schema-defined numeric field and have matching units and a
valid value. Do not infer bindings from vendor quantity names.
Unmatched quantities are preserved under new named resources using map
and its unit factor. An imported physical failure-risk quantity is a
separately named model output or additive count charge, never epsilon,
an operator-norm assertion, or an automatic probability composition rule.
```

Add to §12.3 region records:

```text
model_estimates?:[{model_hash,parameters,root, nodes,outputs,verification}]
```

And add this normative paragraph:

```text
A model estimate is a finite DAG of leaf model invocations and composition
nodes {kind:sequence|parallel|repeat,children,count?}. A repeat count is a
nonnegative integer expression. Children have explicit resource units,
duration and live-interval summaries. Sequence shifts each child's time
origin to its predecessor's end; parallel aligns child origins; repeat
concatenates count copies. Count resources sum, time is makespan, and
physical occupancy and peak are computed from the resulting simultaneous
live intervals, summing simultaneous demands. Shared factories require
explicit shared intervals, not duplicated independent children. Symbolic
results retain their DAG until parameters are bound. These reports are
distinct from required §8.2 resources and MUST NOT be added to the same
leaf counts again. The checker verifies model identity, parameter and unit
bindings, multiplicities and composition, recording checked obligations
separately from external-model assumptions. Unsupported symbolic source
expressions remain preserved external model obligations; they do not
invalidate execution of the object or justify an invented numerical
estimate. A concrete requested estimate with an unresolved obligation is
reported unchecked, with the exact expression and missing model support.
```

The external obligation path preserves the full imported artifact and
symbolic report; it does not claim a checked estimate. Concrete adapter
support remains required work under fd0. Code-distance search belongs to
the estimator/scheduler; its selected value is a resolved target binding,
and its search domain/objective/physical assumptions belong in the model
estimate. The target does not silently choose an optimum.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

Resolved parameters, formulas, input-model identity and bindings change
target_hash. Algorithm-dependent hierarchy changes certificate bytes,
not source closure hashes. No reference catalog leaf needs to change.
Targets, estimator, scheduler and checker must agree on units, exact
evaluation, outward conversion and DAG accounting. Existing count
resources and exact logical-gate epsilon keep their present meanings.
Do not reinterpret a legacy count named failure_probability as a checked
physical-error model.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Check the d=5 example above using rational arithmetic; d=3 gives cycle
18 ns, physical 18 and risk 1/100000. A seconds conversion multiplies
nanoseconds by 1/10^9. Exercise null/bound parameters, odd-distance
validation, forward quantity references, cycles, zero divisors, roots,
incompatible units, unknown binding destinations and a source-hash
mismatch. Test sequence versus parallel peaks, factory sharing and
symbolic repeats. A nonzero risk with exact logical gate epsilon=0 must
leave the logical error bound at zero. The executable arithmetic checks
are in [contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md);
no target consumer was executed here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend **0.10 target/certificate extension**, with a 0.9 K entry
explicitly recording the blocker and the scalar-instance compatibility
construction. The fd0 proposal in the bead is incomplete without binding
destinations, hierarchy and identity. Do not call imported symbolic models
implemented until those vectors and consumer obligations pass. Council
acceptance precedes schema edits, adapter work and any re-pinning.
# Contract derivations — review index and reproducible verification

These are proposals for council grading, not published spec changes.
Contract beads remain open for acceptance/implementation; b5b's owner
handles its implementation status. No git command was run by this job.

A recommendation for a 0.9 K repair means the existing council-reviewed
repair track, not permission to publish an incompatible change silently.
`spec/README.md` normally requires a full-version bump for semantic
changes. Council must settle that publication choice. Every statement
below that existing source hashes stay fixed assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged envelope bytes; a full-version bump
instead changes all symbol/interface/closure envelopes and their consumer
pins. Catalog bytes/hash change only when that separately versioned
catalog is actually changed. No note here performs either publication.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```

- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting

- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## 8. Target descriptions (target 0.9)

### 8.1 Format and expressions

A target is a JSON document in RFC 8785 canonical form; `target_hash` =
SHA-256 of those bytes; numbers are exact rationals from their decimal
text. Cost expressions are a closed JSON AST:

```
  expr := number | {"var": v} | {"op": o, "args": [expr, …]} | {"table": name, "index": "k" | "eps_decade"}
  v    := "k" | "c" | "eps" | "a" | "w" | "i" | "theta"
  o    := "+" (2) | "-" (2) | "*" (2) | "/" (2) | "max" (2) | "min" (2) | "ceil" (1) | "log2inv_eps" (0)
```

`log2inv_eps` = the exact integer min{ j ≥ 0 : eps · 2^j ≥ 1 } for 0 <
eps ≤ 1; eps outside (0, 1] is a load-time error; an entry with
`epsilon` 0 MUST NOT reference `eps` or `log2inv_eps`. Division by zero
and a table index outside the array are load-time errors. Costs MUST be
nonnegative and finite.

**EPS-DECADE** (K9; source: bennett-vm-cxg). A table expression with
`index: "eps_decade"` evaluates its zero-based array index as the exact
integer min{ j ≥ 0 : eps · 10^j ≥ 1 }. It requires a context binding
`eps` and 0 < eps ≤ 1; otherwise it is a load-time error. Thus it uses
the requested `epsilon_share`, not the entry's achieved `epsilon`.
Evaluation uses exact rational comparisons, never a floating-point
logarithm. The existing out-of-array rule applies to this integer. An
entry with `epsilon` 0 MUST NOT use `eps_decade`, including through a
table lookup. This extends the exact-entry prohibition above; it does
not assign an index to eps = 0. Motivation (0.8 §8.1): the grammar names
`"index": "k" | "eps_decade"`, and "Division by zero and a table index
outside the array are load-time errors"; 0.8 defines no decimal index.

**EPS-DECADE witness**

```json
[{"eps":"1","index":0},{"eps":"0.1","index":1},{"eps":"0.1001","index":1},{"eps":"0.0999","index":2},{"eps":"0.001","index":3},{"eps":"0.000999","index":4}]
```

For eps = 999/10000, eps·10 = 999/1000 < 1 and eps·100 = 999/100 ≥ 1,
so the index is 2. A two-entry table rejects this index, a three-entry
table selects element 2. eps = 0, eps > 1 and an unbound eps reject;
no clamping, padding or fallback entry is admitted.

**Variable binding** by evaluation context; referencing a variable that
the context does not bind is a load-time error:

| Context | Bound variables |
|---|---|
| a `gates` entry's `epsilon` and `cost` | `k` = the gate's own control count; `c` = its total control count (`k` + effective control count, §8.3); `eps` = the operation's `epsilon_share` from the schedule (§11) |
| a scheme's `ancillae.count`, template wire subscripts and `repeat` bounds | `k`, `c` as above; `i` inside a `repeat`; `theta` for a parametric scheme |
| a template gate's `params` | `theta`, `i` |
| `qram` costs | `a` = the region's address width A; `w` = its cell width W; `c` |
| a frame descriptor (§9.4) | `c` only |
| `classes.*`, `capabilities.*`, `resources.*.rate` | none (numbers only) |

**Requested versus achieved accuracy.** `eps` is the `epsilon_share`
the schedule assigns to the operation (§11; the default share is the
region's `epsilon` divided by its number of ε > 0 operations). A `gates`
entry whose `epsilon` expression evaluates to a value > `eps` does not
match the operation and matching continues with the next entry; the
error charged to a matched operation is the entry's evaluated `epsilon`;
an entry with `epsilon` 0 is exact and matches every `eps`.

### 8.2 Resources, aggregation, timing

```
  "resources": { "<name>": { "kind": "count"|"time"|"occupancy"|"peak", "unit": "<string>", "rate"?: { "<cls>": n } } }
```

Mandatory: `qubit_second` (occupancy, per class), `time`, `peak_qubits`
(peak, per class). Aggregation from the **scheduled event list** (every
**leaf event** — a native gate after scheme expansion, a classical step,
an effect — with its start time and duration, every wire with its live
interval): `count` sums the per-event costs, each leaf exactly once (a
scheme contributes only its expanded template, never a cost of its own);
`time` is the makespan; `occupancy` integrates, per class, the number of
live wires times the class rate over time (a class absent from `rate`
has rate 1); `peak` is the maximum over time of the number of
concurrently live wires per class (concurrent demands sum). Classical
instructions and effects take `classical_step_time`. Sequential in
program order unless the schedule assigns start times; a gate occupies
its wires for its `time`; gates on disjoint wires MAY overlap unless
`max_parallel` is declared. Connectivity is all-to-all when `topology`
is absent, and is constrained by §8.3 when present (§17 item 15).
Interface wires are charged to the caller for the call's duration;
borrowed wires to the borrower at `dirty_rate`, replacing the owner's
charge.

### 8.3 Classes, gates, schemes, capabilities

```
  "ptr_widths_supported": [32, 64],
  "classes": { "<cls>": { "qubit_second": n, "dirty_rate": n, "capacity": n | "unbounded" } },
  "topology"?: { "vertices": [ { "class": "<cls>" }, … ], "edges": [ [u, v], … ] },
  "gates":   [ { "op": "<gate>", "classes": ["<cls>"|"*", …], "controls": {"min": n, "max": n | "unbounded"},
                 "params": ["theta"]?, "epsilon": expr, "cost": { "<resource>": expr, "time": expr } }, … ],
  "schemes": [ { "name": "<name>", "realises": { "op": "<gate>", "controls": {"min": n, "max": n | "unbounded"}, "params": ["theta"]? },
                 "ancillae": { "count": expr, "state": "clean" | "dirty", "class": "<cls>" },
                 "template": [ { "gate": "<op>", "wires": [ "c[<expr>]" | "t" | "t2" | "a[<expr>]", … ], "params": [expr]?,
                                 "repeat": { "var": "i", "from": expr, "to": expr }? }, … ] }, … ],
                 /* from inclusive, to exclusive; a scheme has no cost or epsilon of its own (§8.2) */
  "qram": null | { "load": { "cost": {…} }, "store": { "cost": {…} }, "exch": { "cost": {…} } },
  "capabilities": { "measurement": {"mid_circuit": bool, "feedforward_latency": n}, "mbu": false,     /* reserved, §6.4 */
                    "max_parallel": n | "unbounded", "max_predicate_depth": n | "unbounded", "classical_step_time": n },
  "objective": { "weighted": { "<resource>": n } } | { "lexicographic": [ "<resource>", … ] },
  "tables": { "<name>": [ n, … ] },
  "imports": { "<model>": { "version": "...", "map": { "<quantity>": ["<resource>", factor] } } }
```

**Matching.** An executed gate with opcode o, operand classes, **total
control count c** (own controls + effective control count) and
parameters matches the first `gates` entry in array order whose `op`,
class pattern and inclusive `controls` range cover it and whose
`epsilon` ≤ `eps` (§8.1); else the first `schemes` entry whose
`realises` covers it; else load-time error. Template wires: `c[e]` the
e-th control (0-based, e an expr over k, c and i) in the operation's
control order (own controls in operand order, then the effective control
context in predicate-stack order, outermost first), `t` the target (`t`
and `t2` the two wires of `swap`/`cz` in operand order), `a[e]` the e-th
ancilla; `params` expressions may use `theta`; `repeat` binds `i` over
[from, to). Every template gate MUST itself match a `gates` entry
(schemes do not nest) and is a leaf event; the scheme's error is the sum
of its template gates' charged errors. Scheme ancillae add to the frame
descriptor. A scheme's correctness is the target author's obligation
(suite E: T3 for permutation schemes, suite D for ε > 0).

**Topology.** `topology` is optional. Its `vertices` array orders the
physical vertices; a vertex's index is its address and its `class` MUST
name a declared addressable class. Each edge is an undirected pair of
integer indices `[u,v]`, with `0 ≤ u < v < vertices.length`; edges MUST
be sorted lexicographically, without duplicates or self-loops. Unknown
fields reject. Each live wire occupies one vertex of its resolved
class, distinct from every other simultaneously live wire. The number
of vertices of a class limits occupancy in addition to its capacity.
A native multi-wire gate is realisable only if every pair of its
operand vertices is joined by an edge (including all effective control
wires); single-wire gates need no edge. Target schemes MUST meet this
rule for each native template leaf, including their ancillae. §11
schedules bind these placements to the target hash. Absent `topology`
means all-to-all with the existing class capacities. Routing and
movement costs are deferred to 0.10 under §17 item 15; placement itself
is validated in 0.9.

**TOPOLOGY witness**

```json
{"capabilities":{"classical_step_time":1,"max_parallel":"unbounded","max_predicate_depth":"unbounded","mbu":false,"measurement":{"feedforward_latency":0,"mid_circuit":false}},"classes":{"fast":{"capacity":4,"dirty_rate":1,"qubit_second":1}},"gates":[{"classes":["fast"],"controls":{"max":1,"min":1},"cost":{"time":1},"epsilon":0,"op":"cx"}],"imports":{},"objective":{"weighted":{"time":1}},"ptr_widths_supported":[32],"qram":null,"resources":{"peak_qubits":{"kind":"peak","unit":"qubit"},"qubit_second":{"kind":"occupancy","unit":"qubit_second"},"time":{"kind":"time","unit":"second"}},"schemes":[],"tables":{},"topology":{"edges":[[0,1],[0,2],[1,3],[2,3]],"vertices":[{"class":"fast"},{"class":"fast"},{"class":"fast"},{"class":"fast"}]}}
```

The vertices are the row-major 2×2 grid; its four edges connect
horizontal and vertical neighbors. A CX on vertices 0 and 1 is
realisable; on 0 and 3 it is infeasible under §17 item 15. The canonical
UTF-8 bytes above exclude the code-fence LF.
Target SHA-256: `960cee71e88da141cc6c2b13cab3f28fce1d50c006e67446aadb592e2bfd0882`.

### 8.4 Space budget

The capacity constraint is peak live qubits per class.

## 9. Objects, identity, linking, reversible ABI (object 0.9)

### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

- CC/R calls: classical frame; callee frames follow the call boundary;
  `uncall` reverses segments (§7.4).
- Region calls: interface bundles and regions passed in declared order,
  caller-owned; callee owns its workspace, returns it clean, local regions
  at image, borrowed bundles returned.
- **Frame descriptor** (certificate): interface slots retain their identities;
  scratch is recorded by class and cleanliness, with widths as expressions
  in inherited control count `c`; retained history is B × frame width for
  the full-history oracle expansion, otherwise the bound library's realised
  history (§17 item 18). Borrowed wires and scheme ancilla frames are
  separate records. Callees `qborrow` only from the caller's designated
  idle set. Shared frames require disjoint live ranges (suite F).

**Frame JSON schema (B5).** The descriptor is the following JCS object;
all five arrays are required, including empty arrays. Every record has
exactly the shown fields; `live_ranges` is optional. Unknown fields at
any depth are errors under §9.1.

```
  { "slots": [ { "id": "<slot identity>", "role": "in"|"tgt"|"fault"|"mut"|"mem_in"|"mem_mut", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "scratch": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "history": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "borrow": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "ancillae": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "scheme": "<scheme name>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "live_ranges"?: [ { "frame": "<frame identity>", "start": ordinal, "end": ordinal }, … ] }
```

`slots` lists the source interface in operand order: oracle `in`, `tgt`,
`fault`, then regions; action `mut`, `in`, then regions. Slot identities
are `<group>:<zero-based index within group>` (`in:0`, `tgt:0`,
`fault:0`, `mut:0`, `mem:0`, …); a region uses role `mem_in` or
`mem_mut`. A region slot's width counts all its cells, in address then
bit order; bundle bits are least-significant first. Slot widths MUST
match the signature. Inherited controls are caller-owned and precede
these slots in §11 topology placement; they are not renamed callee
slots. Every class is a resolved target class. Non-slot frame identities
are nonempty producer-chosen strings, distinct from every other record
identity in the descriptor; a dynamic identity additionally includes
the invocation context and the expansion scratch identity (§11.1).
Array order is source interface order for slots, and first allocation
order in the realised expansion for each other array. `scratch` records
per-class, per-cleanliness workspace; `history` records retained history;
`borrow` records dirty requirements; `ancillae` records each scheme's
ancilla frame and its declared state. These categories do not double
count the same wires. No requirements may be omitted because they vanish
for one chosen basis input.

Every `expr` uses §8.1's expression language with **only `c` bound**;
other variables and table lookups (whose indices require `k` or `eps`)
are invalid. At every covered inherited control count the widths MUST
be nonnegative integers; interface widths are positive and signature
fixed. Instantiation substitutes the resolved budget, parameters and
expansion choices into constants before forming these expressions.
Widths describe the actual realisation, including history and scheme
ancillae, and are checked against it. Each `live_ranges` entry names a
non-slot frame identity and a half-open interval `[start,end)` of its
invocation's generated leaf order (including step/phase/path expansion,
§11); `start < end`. A shared frame identity retains its recorded class, state and width
across uses; every use of shared storage MUST be listed and intervals
of the same frame MUST be pairwise disjoint. An omitted list gives no shared-storage permission.

**FRAME witness**

```json
{"ancillae":[{"class":"fast","id":"a","scheme":"controlled-x","state":"clean","width":{"var":"c"}}],"borrow":[{"class":"fast","id":"b","width":1}],"history":[{"class":"fast","id":"h","width":3}],"live_ranges":[{"end":2,"frame":"s","start":0},{"end":4,"frame":"s","start":2}],"scratch":[{"class":"fast","id":"s","state":"clean","width":{"args":[2,{"var":"c"}],"op":"+"}}],"slots":[{"class":"fast","id":"mut:0","role":"mut","width":2},{"class":"fast","id":"in:0","role":"in","width":1}]}
```

At `c = 2`, the slot widths total 3, scratch width is 4, retained
history width is 3, borrow width is 1 and scheme ancilla width is 2.
The two half-open uses of `s` meet at 2 and do not overlap. This is a
schema/arithmetic witness; a real certificate must also bind the
expansion and target defining `controlled-x`. Its canonical UTF-8
bytes exclude the code-fence LF.
Frame SHA-256: `f314eeef24a5a65a9c72f91e062aa3c83556358eaca166e689571700a72083ff`.

## 10. Launch ABI (launch 0.9)

 succeeded in 117ms:
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Read-only finite witnesses for the rev2 proposals, not an ISA/format checker.

Uses only this repository's published catalog routine/data and the eight notes.
No subprocesses, network, git, temporary files, tracker or source mutations.
"""
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from copy import deepcopy
from math import isqrt, ceil, floor
import hashlib, json, re
ROOT=Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT=ROOT/'docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2'
text=(OUT/'fd0.md').read_text()
entry=json.loads(text.split('<!-- import-example -->\n```json\n',1)[1].split('\n```',1)[0])
m=entry['model']
def q(x):
    assert isinstance(x,str)
    v=F(x)
    assert str(v)==x, ('noncanonical rational',x)
    return v
def dims(d):return {k:q(v) for k,v in d.items() if q(v)}
def dimcombine(a,b,sign=1):
    out=dict(a)
    for k,v in b.items():out[k]=out.get(k,F(0))+sign*v
    return {k:v for k,v in out.items() if v}
def unit(n):
    u=m['units'][n]
    assert set(u)=={'dimension','scale'} and q(u['scale'])>0
    return q(u['scale']),dims(u['dimension'])
class Unbound(Exception):pass

def ev(e,params,seen=()):
    if isinstance(e,str):return q(e),{}
    assert isinstance(e,dict)
    if set(e)=={'lit','unit'}:
        scale,d=unit(e['unit']); return q(e['lit'])*scale,d
    if set(e)=={'param'}:
        p=m['parameters'][e['param']]; x=params[e['param']]
        if x is None:raise Unbound(e['param'])
        v,d=ev(x,{},seen); assert not d
        scale,d=unit(p['unit']); return v*scale,d
    if set(e)=={'quantity'}:
        n=e['quantity']; assert n not in seen, ('cycle',n)
        v,d=ev(m['quantities'][n]['expr'],params,seen+(n,))
        assert d==unit(m['quantities'][n]['unit'])[1]
        return v,d
    if set(e)=={'external'}:raise Unbound(e['external']['location'])
    assert set(e)=={'op','args'}
    op=e['op']; a=e['args']
    assert op in ['+','-','*','/','min','max','ceil','sqrt','pow']
    assert len(a)==(1 if op in ['ceil','sqrt'] else 2)
    x,dx=ev(a[0],params,seen)
    if op=='ceil':assert not dx; return F(ceil(x)),{}
    if op=='sqrt':
        assert x>=0
        n,d=isqrt(x.numerator),isqrt(x.denominator)
        assert n*n==x.numerator and d*d==x.denominator, 'nonrational root: use exact algebraic AST/enclosure'
        return F(n,d),{k:v/2 for k,v in dx.items()}
    y,dy=ev(a[1],params,seen)
    if op in ['+','-','min','max']:
        assert dx==dy, ('unit mismatch',dx,dy)
        return {'+':lambda:x+y,'-':lambda:x-y,'min':lambda:min(x,y),'max':lambda:max(x,y)}[op](),dx
    if op=='*':return x*y,dimcombine(dx,dy)
    if op=='/':assert y!=0; return x/y,dimcombine(dx,dy,-1)
    assert not dy and y.denominator==1
    assert x!=0 or y>0
    return x**int(y),{k:v*y for k,v in dx.items()}

def defaults():return {k:p['value'] for k,p in m['parameters'].items()}
def validate_param(p,x):
    if x is None:return
    v,d=ev(x,{})
    assert not d
    if p['type']=='integer':assert v.denominator==1
    dom=p['domain']
    assert dom['min'] is None or v>=q(dom['min'])
    assert dom['max'] is None or v<=q(dom['max'])
    assert dom['parity']=='any' or (p['type']=='integer' and int(v)%2=={'odd':1,'even':0}[dom['parity']])
    if p['role']=='code_distance':assert p['type']=='integer' and unit(p['unit'])== (F(1),{}) and q(dom['min'])>=1

def fails(call):
    try:call()
    except (AssertionError,ZeroDivisionError,KeyError,Unbound):return
    raise AssertionError('negative vector did not fail')
assert set(entry)=={'version','map','model'}

## 10. Launch ABI (launch 0.9)

**Model.** A run executes a device CC entry; the host passes classical
arguments, register ids and the entropy tape, receives classical
results, fault codes and declared measurements; host services are
reached by `hostcall`. **Positional binding**: argument i of `bennett_
run` binds to parameter i of the entry's signature: `INT(width, bytes)`
to `iN` with width = N (else `E_ARG`), `F64` to `f64`, `QREG` to `qreg[n]`
with the register's n or to `qreg[*]` with any width, `TOKEN` to `token`
(a valid token, §5.4). Arguments and the entropy tape
are **copied** at `bennett_run`; the caller may free its buffers
afterwards. Host-minted tokens are scoped to the program and destroyed
at unload; device-minted tokens to their run (§5.4).
Accessor-returned pointers are valid until the owning object is freed.
`bennett_unload` with a run not CLOSED: `E_INVALID_STATE`.

**Invocation tree.** A run record is a tree (CC segments, R calls, Q
calls); an R call's subtree is owned by its R history; `bennett_reverse`
reverses post-order: CC segments by their run history, R calls by LIFO
reversal of their history (§7.4, reversed segments and `uncall` frames
skipped), top-level Q calls by re-application / adjoint. A fault record
is terminal (§4.9): the run is `FAILED`, and every activation open at
the fault keeps its frames, which reversal undoes like those of a call
that returned.

**States.** `CREATED → RUNNING → (SUSPENDED ⇄ RUNNING) → (HOST_REQUEST ⇄
RUNNING) → COMPLETED | FAILED`; `COMPLETED → REVERSED`; `FAILED →
REVERSED`; any of
`COMPLETED/FAILED/REVERSED → CLOSED` (`bennett_run_close` releases the
record; register ids created by the run remain valid unless measured or
freed). A run owns the registers and memory it touches exclusively while
not CLOSED; a register reached by `qreg.from_id` is touched from then
on; `bennett_qreg_prepare`, `bennett_qreg_free` and a `bennett_run`
argument naming a register owned by another run that is not CLOSED are
`E_INVALID_STATE`. **Reverse eligibility**: `COMPLETED` or `FAILED`; no `measure`
frame; no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified (§17
item 14); no register it touched has been prepared, freed, sliced or
used by another run since; else `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`. Reversing a `FAILED`
run first undoes its terminal fault record, then traverses the rest of
its history exactly as for a completed run, including the frames of
activations that never returned; the run is then `REVERSED` with every
component T4 names at its entry value. `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_
qreg_free` require a clean register (`E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` otherwise on a
verifying executor; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14); there
is no reset and no trace-out: a register in unknown state is released by
a `measure` inside a run, which consumes its handle (§3.3).

**Results.** `bennett_result_get` writes value i and sets `*kind` and
`*width`: `INT` as ⌈N/8⌉ little-endian bytes, `*width` = N; `F64` as the
8 IEEE 754 binary64 little-endian bytes, `*width` = 64; `QREG` and
`TOKEN` as the 8-byte little-endian id, `*width` = 64. A `cap` smaller
than that byte count is `E_ARG` and nothing is written. Fault records
are read by `bennett_result_nfaults` / `bennett_result_fault` (codes of
§4.9; a `COMPLETED` run has none, a `FAILED` run has exactly one, its
terminal record, and exposes no values).
For an R `fault C`, `bennett_wait` returns `OK` with `*result_out` set
and `*request_out` NULL as for any `FAILED` run;
`bennett_result_nvalues` is 0, `bennett_result_nfaults` is 1 and
`bennett_result_fault(result, 0)` is exactly C when C ≠ 0 (4 when C = 0,
§5.4), with all 64 bits preserved. The fault code is result data, not a
`bennett_status` error or a host-service reply.

**Waiting.** `bennett_wait` blocks until the run leaves `RUNNING` and
returns `OK` with exactly one outcome: `HOST_REQUEST` (`*request_out`
set, `*result_out` NULL; `bennett_reply` answers it and the run is
`RUNNING` again) or `COMPLETED` / `FAILED` (`*result_out` set, `*request_
out` NULL). Waiting on a `SUSPENDED`, `REVERSED` or `CLOSED` run is `E_
INVALID_STATE`.

**`hostcall`.** `rlen = hostcall service ptr len`: `[ptr, ptr + len)` MUST
lie in one live allocation (else CC trap and no request is made); those
bytes are copied into the request; the run enters `HOST_REQUEST`; the
reply payload is written to `[ptr, ptr + min(len, reply length))` and
`rlen` = the full reply length (so truncation is detectable; a reply
whose length does not fit `ptr_width` bits is refused by `bennett_reply`
with `E_ARG` before any write, and the run stays in `HOST_REQUEST`); the frame
records the request bytes, the overwritten bytes and the reply bytes.
Every `service` is declared in `devices.services`; an undeclared one is
a load error.

**Tape.** `bennett_tape_next` yields each `effect` in tape order; `args`
holds the operation's operands in the order of §7.3, each an unsigned
64-bit little-endian field (pointers zero-extended from `ptr_width`), so
`*alen` = 8 × arity.

```c
/* launch 0.9. All objects opaque. A function that returns an object transfers ownership to the caller,
   released with the matching *_free. Status 0 = BENNETT_OK. */
typedef enum { BENNETT_OK=0, BENNETT_E_VERSION, BENNETT_E_PTR_WIDTH, BENNETT_E_DEVICE_MISSING, BENNETT_E_GATE_UNSUPPORTED,
