### 17.7 The native comparison build

Differential testing (NS §5.1) compares the artefact with the **native comparison binary**: the
materialised device variants, code-generated by the same LLVM major and linked with
`bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` (never host libc or libm) under the comparison link of
§19.5. It is never an `-O0` build: optimisation legally changes marker operands (spike F4), and
the materialised optimised module is the one program both builds implement. The driver MUST make
division by zero and INT_MIN/−1 trap in the native build: on targets whose divide instruction does
not trap it inserts, after materialisation and outside the materialised module, a check that
calls `__bennett_nz_fault` with code 5 or 6. Results compare by the outcome table of §18.7.

**Comparison-only support exception.** Only after the materialised device module has been fixed, the native comparison backend MAY define the three `__bennett_nz_` primitives of §3.1 as native support, and the driver MUST emit `__bennett_rt_native_layout` as specified in §19.5; these definitions MUST NOT enter producer inputs, production device bitcode, region identity or the materialised module used by the ISA map.

The native primitive support MUST reproduce the resolved device addresses, zero fill, live allocation sizes, nonreclaiming allocation/free history and fault outcomes of §§17.1/17.5, with capacity for every history admitted by the resolved heap budget (at most one heap record per byte since max(n,1) ≥ 1), without an arbitrary smaller record cap; host setup/resource failure MUST terminate comparison with an explicit infrastructure failure, never silently relocate the heap, choose defaults, use host malloc, or report a successful dialect outcome.

**Definedness classifiers.** The comparison binary MUST detect the materialised memory faults of §17.5 (out-of-live-allocation access, use after free and invalid/double free), division by zero and signed division overflow, and reached explicit fault/unreachable paths; instrumentation or a checked memory adapter may supply these checks outside the materialised module, and UBSan-only coverage MUST NOT be reported as satisfying the memory-fault obligation.

The harness MUST separately classify source inputs using instrumented original-input builds or a checked reference interpreter as in NS §3.2, report flagged inputs as undefined/excluded, and record the classifier's actual detection coverage; no classifier claims source UB erased by optimisation is detectable in the comparison binary.

## 18. Reference semantics

### 18.1 State

The reference semantics runs H functions natively and every device entry, region invocation and
whole-program entry on the materialised module over a state Σ = (C, Q, H, G, K, T):

- **C**, the classical state: LLVM's semantics of the materialised module (LangRef), with every
  remaining choice fixed by §12–§14 and the fault outcomes of §17.5; it includes the shadows of
  the arena (TDL-4). In particular GEP uses the pointer-index width, as fixed
  in §16.4 and §17.4, also in constant expressions; pointer representations and
  conversions use P(a), not an implicit pw-bit move;
- **Q**, the quantum state of the ISA formal model (ISA §14.2): a unit vector over the live wires
  in allocation order, including the arena's wires (TDL-4);
- **H**, the handle table: for each handle, its wires, whether it is live, and the
  generation it was allocated in, if any;
- **G**, a stack of **recorders**, one per active generation (§18.4);
- **K**, the coherence states of the object instances (TDL-2);
- **T**, the device-model states and the effect tape (§11).


 succeeded in 37ms:
### 19.4 Placement: host vs device CC

H functions stay native unless they share **pointer-rich** memory with device code: a pointer
argument of a device-entry call, or a global accessed by both, whose pointee may contain pointers
that device code reads (§5). Such H functions are **promoted** to CC variants, transitively up to
their roots (in the limit `main`, and then H is the system-call proxy only: `hostcall
bennett.syscall`). Results are equal by the materialised semantics; ISA-11 would let them stay
native (an efficiency and placement item, §25.3; outline E11).

### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off; the null region [0,
`null_size`) with `null_size` = 4096; the data sections (`.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order)
from `data_base` = 4096; the text from `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when the device ranges below
(data, heap, arena, interface regions, computed first) end above 0x400000, from the first multiple
of 0x200000 at or after their end, so that the native comparison binary maps its code disjointly
from them; the defaults are options (`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`,
§24). The link map gives A(F) for
every function and the base of every global. The device `layout` (ISA §9.1) replicates it:

- `globals`: every global of the link at its link address with its image;
- `heap_base`: the first multiple of 4096 at or after the end of the data; `heap_limit_max`: the
  largest `heap` of the budget table (which covers the shadow stack, §9.2);
- the arena (TDL-4) at the first multiple of its size 2^A at or after the heap's end, with 2^A the
  smallest power of two covering the may-Co globals and the coherent allocator's heap (its size
  from `--bennett-arena-heap`, default the largest `heap` of the table); the arena's shadow
  `__bennett_rt_shadow` is a global of the data sections;
- interface regions per oracle above the arena (§16.3); H, A_tab and S_cells by §16.3;
- the production host image is linked with the same addresses for every function and global
  whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4).

*(non-normative)* With data low and text high, H covers the data, heap and regions; function
addresses are values only and never occupy device memory.

**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.

### 19.6 Build-time snapshots

`--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` rewrites the initialisers of the mutable globals listed in the dump
(and the platform RAM global, §20.1) to the dump's bytes before the plugin runs; the data images
then carry the snapshot and R of §8.5 is empty for them (rung 7: "kernel state snapshot fixed
classically after boot"). The dump is recorded by hash in the build record.

**Quantum registers in CC and R.** A **register** is n device-resident
wires of one class with a nonzero 64-bit **id**, recorded in the
program's **register ledger** with its class, width and state:
**live**, **viewed** (one slice of it is live) or **consumed**.
`qreg[n]` is the type of a register whose width n is fixed statically;
`qreg[*]` the type of a register whose width is known only at run time
(an unsigned 64-bit n ≥ 0). A `qreg[n]` value may be used wherever a
`qreg[*]` is expected; a `qreg[*]` value bound to a `qreg[n]` parameter
or block parameter (`call`, `br`, `ret`, launch) or to a slot of width n
(region call, §4.7) is checked at that transfer: its width MUST equal
the required one. The `qreg.*` instructions execute in CC and R;
`measure` only in CC. An instruction whose precondition fails is a CC
trap / R fault (§4.9) with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` unless the table says
otherwise, and has no effect. An id's value is chosen by the executor
(the suites compare ids only for equality); an id is never reassigned to
another register while the program is loaded. **Handle transitions:**

| Instruction | Precondition | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `q = qreg.new cls n` | `n` a literal n ≥ 1 (q: `qreg[n]`) or an `i64` value read unsigned, 0 included (q: `qreg[*]`); the device can provide n wires of class `cls`, else code `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | q live, clean, with a fresh id and wires disjoint from every live or viewed register. [deps: ISA-3.3-P7] |
| `qreg.free q` | q live, not a view, clean on every basis configuration (a verifying executor checks it; on failure q stays live and unchanged; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14) | q consumed |
| `q2 = qreg.slice q lo len` | q live, not a view; `lo` and `len` each a literal (`len` ≥ 1) or an `i64` value, both unsigned; lo + len ≤ n, computed without wrap (a trap even when literals make the failure evident) | q2 a view of wires [lo, lo+len) of q, of type `qreg[len]` for a literal len and `qreg[*]` otherwise; q viewed; q2 MUST be the operand of exactly one region call and of nothing else; after that call q2 is consumed and q is live again; a use of q while it is viewed traps (the static rule below covers the cases suite F rejects). Reversing that region call (§7.4) restores q viewed and q2 live; reversing the slice frame then restores q live |
| `q = qreg.concat q1 q2` | q1, q2 live, not views, distinct registers | q1, q2 consumed; q live, with a fresh id, over the wires of q1 then q2; `qreg[n1+n2]` if both operands are static, else `qreg[*]` |
| `w = qreg.width q` | q live, not a view | w: `i64`, the width; no state change |
| `id = qreg.id q` | q live, not a view | id: `i64`, the bits of q's id; no state change |
| `q = qreg.from_id id` | `id` is the id of a register of the program that is live, not a view, and not owned by another run that is not CLOSED (§10) | q: `qreg[*]`, an alias of that register; no ledger state change; the run now owns the register (§10), which reversal does not undo (ownership ends when the run is CLOSED) |
| region call (§4.7) with q as operand | q live (a view included); its width equals the slot's (§3.3 binding); the wire sets filling distinct wire/region slots are pairwise disjoint, and an explicit control is disjoint from targets and consumed/produced operands (read-only input overlap remains allowed), else CC trap / R fault FAULT_UB_TRAP before any effect, including any view consumption. [deps: ISA-3.3-P4, ISA-4.7, ISA-4.9] | q unchanged. [deps: ISA-4.7] |
| `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | q live, not a view; the form `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) for a `qreg[*]`, whose width MUST be N (checked before measuring); for a `qreg[n]` either `measure` or `measure.iN` with N = n (checked statically) | q consumed, its wires released; r: `in` / `iN` |
| reversal of a `qreg.*` frame (§7.4, §10) | – | the ledger state before that frame (below). A `measure` frame is **never reversed**: a segment containing one cannot be uncalled (§7.4) and a run containing one is not reversible (§10) |

**Ledger frames and their reversal** (§7.4). A `qreg.new` frame records
the id, class and width; its reversal releases that register without a
test: every later frame touching it has been undone first (LIFO) and the
run owns it exclusively (§10), so under T4's exactness hypothesis it is
clean, and otherwise its residual, within the declared bounds of the
undone realisations (§12.3), is discarded with it.
A `qreg.free` frame records the id, class, width and whether its
cleanliness was verified; its reversal re-creates the register clean and
live with the same id, class and width (every alias sees it live
again). A `qreg.slice` frame records the parent id, lo and len, a
`qreg.concat` frame the two operand ids and the new id; their reversal
restores the exact prior states and wire order without moving, copying
or measuring a wire. `qreg.width`, `qreg.id` and `qreg.from_id` change
no ledger state and their frames are pure (§7.4); if an `uncall` later
removes the register an alias refers to, every use of the alias traps.

**Binding**: a register of width n (`qreg[n]`, or `qreg[*]` of run-time
width n) fills a `wire[n]` slot bit for bit (bit 0 ↔ wire 0); it fills a
`region[A,W]` slot iff n = 2^A·W with cell i = bits [iW, (i+1)W); for a
`qreg[*]` the width is checked at the call, before any effect.
Host-supplied register ids are `qreg` values of the parameter's type.

The register ids supplied as arguments of one `bennett_run` MUST
denote pairwise disjoint registers, else `bennett_run` returns `E_ARG`
before executing the entry function or acquiring any register ownership.
[deps: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-10]

**Registers, handles and aliases.** A `qreg` value is a reference to a
register of the device; the state live / viewed / consumed belongs to
the register, not to a name. `qreg` values pass unchanged as `call`
arguments, `ret` values and `br` block arguments (each transfer binds
another name to the same register and changes no state; a `qreg[*]`
reaching a `qreg[n]` parameter is width-checked, above). Two names bound
to one register (by such transfers or by `qreg.from_id`) are
**aliases**: a transition
made through either is observed through both. The wire operands of one
region call, with its effective control context, MUST denote pairwise
disjoint wires (§3.3 disjointness); aliases violate it. **Static and
run-time verdicts.** Suite F rejects exactly: two `qreg` operands of one
region call or of one `qreg.concat` that are the same name; and, on some path from a
`qreg.slice`, a use of the view's name other than as the operand of one
region call (a `br`, `call` or `ret` transfer included), its reaching a
`ret` or the end of the function unconsumed, or a use of the slice's
parent operand name before that call. Every other overlap, and every
use of a viewed register through another name, is a CC trap / R fault
with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

- CC/R calls: classical frame; callee frames follow the call boundary;
  `uncall` reverses segments (§7.4).
- Region calls: interface bundles and regions passed in declared order,
  caller-owned; callee owns its workspace, returns it clean, local regions
  at image, borrowed bundles returned.
- **Frame descriptor** (certificate): interface slots retain their identities;
  scratch is recorded by class and cleanliness, with widths as expressions
  in inherited control count `c`; retained history is B × frame width for
  the full-history oracle expansion, otherwise the bound library's realised
  history (§17 item 18). Borrowed wires and scheme ancilla frames are
  separate records. Callees `qborrow` only from the caller's designated
  idle set. Shared frames require disjoint live ranges (suite F).

**Frame JSON schema (B5).** The descriptor is the following JCS object;
all five arrays are required, including empty arrays. Every record has
exactly the shown fields; `live_ranges` is optional. Unknown fields at
any depth are errors under §9.1.

```
  { "slots": [ { "id": "<slot identity>", "role": "in"|"tgt"|"fault"|"mut"|"mem_in"|"mem_mut", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "scratch": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "history": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "borrow": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "ancillae": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "scheme": "<scheme name>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "live_ranges"?: [ { "frame": "<frame identity>", "start": ordinal, "end": ordinal }, … ] }
```

`slots` lists the source interface in operand order: oracle `in`, `tgt`,
`fault`, then regions; action `mut`, `in`, then regions. Slot identities
are `<group>:<zero-based index within group>` (`in:0`, `tgt:0`,
`fault:0`, `mut:0`, `mem:0`, …); a region uses role `mem_in` or
`mem_mut`. A region slot's width counts all its cells, in address then
bit order; bundle bits are least-significant first. Slot widths MUST
match the signature. Inherited controls are caller-owned and precede
these slots in §11 topology placement; they are not renamed callee
slots. Every class is a resolved target class. Non-slot frame identities
are nonempty producer-chosen strings, distinct from every other record
identity in the descriptor; a dynamic identity additionally includes
the invocation context and the expansion scratch identity (§11.1).
Array order is source interface order for slots, and first allocation
order in the realised expansion for each other array. `scratch` records
per-class, per-cleanliness workspace; `history` records retained history;
`borrow` records dirty requirements; `ancillae` records each scheme's
ancilla frame and its declared state. These categories do not double
count the same wires. No requirements may be omitted because they vanish
for one chosen basis input.

Every `expr` uses §8.1's expression language with **only `c` bound**;
other variables and table lookups (whose indices require `k` or `eps`)
are invalid. At every covered inherited control count the widths MUST
be nonnegative integers; interface widths are positive and signature
fixed. Instantiation substitutes the resolved budget, parameters and
expansion choices into constants before forming these expressions.
Widths describe the actual realisation, including history and scheme
ancillae, and are checked against it. Each `live_ranges` entry names a
non-slot frame identity and a half-open interval `[start,end)` of its
invocation's generated leaf order (including step/phase/path expansion,
§11); `start < end`. A shared frame identity retains its recorded class, state and width
across uses; every use of shared storage MUST be listed and intervals
of the same frame MUST be pairwise disjoint. An omitted list gives no shared-storage permission.

**FRAME witness**

```json
{"ancillae":[{"class":"fast","id":"a","scheme":"controlled-x","state":"clean","width":{"var":"c"}}],"borrow":[{"class":"fast","id":"b","width":1}],"history":[{"class":"fast","id":"h","width":3}],"live_ranges":[{"end":2,"frame":"s","start":0},{"end":4,"frame":"s","start":2}],"scratch":[{"class":"fast","id":"s","state":"clean","width":{"args":[2,{"var":"c"}],"op":"+"}}],"slots":[{"class":"fast","id":"mut:0","role":"mut","width":2},{"class":"fast","id":"in:0","role":"in","width":1}]}
```

At `c = 2`, the slot widths total 3, scratch width is 4, retained
history width is 3, borrow width is 1 and scheme ancilla width is 2.
The two half-open uses of `s` meet at 2 and do not overlap. This is a
schema/arithmetic witness; a real certificate must also bind the
expansion and target defining `controlled-x`. Its canonical UTF-8
bytes exclude the code-fence LF.
Frame SHA-256: `f314eeef24a5a65a9c72f91e062aa3c83556358eaca166e689571700a72083ff`.

Native comparison uses the materialised outlined-region callee closure required by the dialect contract. This extension requires no host-shim implementation." Add a consumer example with one native host image and one marked device module, whose report binds that module's certificate and closure.
- **[codex] B1** (Proposed §8.5 objective selectors and Reports; interaction with §11; contract): *Fix:* Extend §8.5 and §11 with: “For each model-resource objective term, the scheduling request supplies one model_inputs entry keyed by that resource. The entry contains the selected root, complete model parameter bindings and shared reservations. Its root MUST equal the selector's model/node. The entry is serialized in schedule.bindings and participates in schedule_hash. Objective evaluation uses this entry and the resolved target only. A report certifying the objective MUST reproduce those inputs and bind the same invocation/context; other analytical reports cannot supply implicit inputs.” Missing inputs produce an explicit incomplete-model-objective diagnostic, not an invented value. For the witness, the 50-qubit input yields peak 50 and occupancy 9/2000000; the 100-qubit input yields 100 and 9/1000000, under distinct schedule hashes. An alternative is to move these inputs into resolved target bytes and change target_hash; I recommend schedule binding to preserve the proposal's separation of algorithm studies from hardware identity.
  <1>1 A model-resource objective selects a hierarchy metric under current algorithm bindings. Shared reservations affect occupancy and peak, and are supplied by a Report rather than the target model.  — clause:proposed §8.5 Scalar and unit types; clause:proposed §8.5 Hierarchy node grammar
  <1>2 Selectors identify model/node/metric but no evaluation-input or report identity. Multiple model_estimates are permitted. The proposed publication edits add no model input to the §11 request or schedule bindings.  — witness:docs/notes/2026-11-02-textdefects/fd0-import-text.md:108; witness:docs/notes/2026-11-02-textdefects/fd0-import-text.md:380; clause:ISA 0.9 §11
  <1>3 Use the worked three-sequential-use shared-factory variant. Two reports can have the same target, subject, root and parameters, but reserve respectively 50 and 100 physical qubits for 90 ns with one slot. Both satisfy the stated reservation/accounting rules. Their factory peaks are 50 and 100; their occupancies are 9/2000000 and 9/1000000 physical-qubit seconds. A peak selector has identical serialized fields in both cases.  — witness:docs/notes/2026-11-02-textdefects/fd0-import-text.md, shared-factory variant; clause:proposed §8.5 Shared resources
  <1>4 The new objective mechanism does not determine which value belongs to the scheduling problem. Choosing an analytical report, reservation set or parameter environment is a missing semantic input, not an implementation choice about numerical evaluation.  — step:<1>1; step:<1>2; step:<1>3
- **[codex] B2** (Proposed §8.5 objective selectors; Conformance vectors, final row; contract): *Fix:* In §8.5 state: “A missing required model_outputs selector is malformed input, not an unbound expression.” Replace the vector with three cases: complete selector and concrete inputs → evaluated scalar; complete selector with free dependent parameters → symbolic unresolved objective; missing selector → validation error at objective.model_outputs.<resource>. Alternatively, explicitly admit incomplete selector templates and revise the MUST rule, but I recommend retaining the closed-schema rule. None of these validation outcomes is an ISA fault.
  <2>1 Every model-resource objective term MUST have exactly one selector; malformed typed input produces a validation error.  — clause:proposed §8.5 Scalar and unit types; clause:proposed §8.5 Reports, identity and checking
  <2>2 The final conformance vector instead requires an unresolved objective when the selector is absent.  — witness:docs/notes/2026-11-02-textdefects/fd0-import-text.md:847
  <2>3 For a declared model resource model_cycle, objective={weighted:{model_cycle:1}} violates the mandatory-selector rule, yet the vector treats it as unresolved rather than malformed.  — step:<2>1; step:<2>2
- **[codex] B3** (Corresponding §12.3 insertion; Consumer impact and release; contract): The proposal changes certificate layout but never states how the new reports apply to a mixed fat object. References to an object's closure and containing certificate do not explicitly preserve native host code plus only outlined device modules. This fails the council's mandatory mixed-fat-object standing check. *Fix:* Add to §12.3: “In a mixed fat object, model_estimates attach to the certificate of the identified linked device ISA object and its outlined region closure. object_hash and closure_hash here identify that device object and closure, not the enclosing native binary. The fat-object inventory associates each device object with its certificate and target-bound reports; native host code remains outside these device resource reports. Adding a model estimate does not require lowering the host entry point, unrelated native functions, or host-library closure into ISA objects, and does not claim a whole-native-program resource total.” Repeat this scope in the consumer analysis.

## sol

The cited 0.9 import gap is real, the worked arithmetic and fixture hashes reproduce, and the risk/error separation is sound. Revise five contract issues: operational rebinding, standalone identity, selector verdicts, catalog migration and mixed-fat-object coverage. The core remains acceptable; the 0.10 route can preserve current concrete estimate capabilities.

**Path to accept:** First freeze operational parameter dependencies against report/invoke rebinding. Then complete standalone report subjects with variant and inherited controls. Correct the missing-selector verdict, specify the ref-library migration for full-version 0.10, and add the mixed-fat-object statement and example. These changes suffice without implementing adapters, a general algebraic evaluator or proof machinery in this round.

- **C1 5**: The insertion preserves the machine state and step relation. Model-validation failures remain separate from ISA faults. *Change:* No change required. Retain §12.3's sentence: "No model output changes the object's instruction semantics."
